S_{(d)}\times{\mathbb{R}}
of
f|_{M_{d}}
, we deduce that
f|_{S_{(d)}}
is semialgebraic. Observe that
S^{\prime}:=S\setminus S_{(d)}
is an open semialgebraic subset of
S
of strictly smaller dimension. In addition,
{\mathcal{J}}_{S}|_{S^{\prime}}={\mathcal{J}}_{S^{\prime}}
and by induction hypothesis (on the dimension), we deduce
f|_{S^{\prime}}
is semialgebraic (in the case of dimension
0
all the maps are semialgebraic). Consequently,
f
is a semialgebraic map.

(iii)
\Longrightarrow
(i) As
S
is locally compact,
C:=\operatorname{Cl}(S)\setminus S
is a closed semialgebraic subset of
{\mathbb{R}}^{n}
. As for each
x\in S
there exists a Nash function germ
F_{x}\in{\mathcal{N}}_{{\mathbb{R}}^{n},x}
such that
F_{x}|_{S_{x}}=f_{x}
, there exist an open neighborhood
U_{0}\subset{\mathbb{R}}^{n}
of
S
and a
{\mathcal{C}}^{\infty}
function
F_{0}:U_{0}\to{\mathbb{R}}
such that
F_{0}|_{S}=f
. Let
\sigma_{0}:{\mathbb{R}}^{n}\setminus C\to[0,1]
be a
{\mathcal{C}}^{\infty}
bump function such that
\sigma_{0}|_{S}=1
and
\sigma_{0}|_{({\mathbb{R}}^{n}\setminus C)\setminus U_{0}}=0
and define
F:=\sigma_{0}\cdot F_{0}:{\mathbb{R}}^{n}\setminus C\to[0,1]
, which is a
{\mathcal{C}}^{\infty}
function on
{\mathbb{R}}^{n}\setminus C
such that
F|_{S}=f
.

By Mostowski’s Lemma
[
Mo
, Lem.6]
there exists a continuous semialgebraic function
h:{\mathbb{R}}^{n}\to{\mathbb{R}}
such that
\mathcal{Z}(h)=C
and
h|_{{\mathbb{R}}^{n}\setminus C}
is a Nash function. Consider the Nash diffeomorphism

\varphi:{\mathbb{R}}^{n}\setminus C\to M:=\{(x,t)\in{\mathbb{R}}^{n+1}:\ h(x)t-1=0\}\subset{\mathbb{R}}^{n+1}.

By
[
BCR
, Cor.8.9.5]
there exist an open semialgebraic neighborhood
U
of
M
in
{\mathbb{R}}^{n+1}
and a Nash retraction
\rho:U\to M
. Let
\sigma:{\mathbb{R}}^{n+1}\to[0,1]
be a
{\mathcal{C}}^{\infty}
bump function such that
\sigma|_{M}=1
and
\sigma|_{{\mathbb{R}}^{n+1}\setminus U}=0
. Thus,
g:=\sigma\cdot(F\circ\varphi^{-1}\circ\rho):{\mathbb{R}}^{n+1}\to{\mathbb{R}}
is a
{\mathcal{C}}^{\infty}
differentiable function on
{\mathbb{R}}^{n+1}
(if we extend it by
0
outside
U
). If
T:=\varphi(S)
, we have that
g|_{T}=f\circ\varphi^{-1}|_{T}
is semialgebraic. By
[
BCM
, Cor.1.7]
for each
p\geq 1
there exists an
{\mathcal{S}}^{p}
function
G_{p}:{\mathbb{R}}^{n+1}\to{\mathbb{R}}
such that
G_{p}|_{T}=f\circ\varphi^{-1}|_{T}
. Define
F_{p}:=G_{p}\circ\varphi:{\mathbb{R}}^{n}\setminus C\to{\mathbb{R}}
, which is a
{\mathcal{S}}^{p}
function such that
F_{p}|_{S}=f
for each
p\geq 1
. Thus,
f\in\bigcap_{p\geq 0}{\mathcal{S}}^{p}(S)={\mathcal{S}}^{(\infty)}(S)
, as required.
∎

Remark 7.11
.

The previous result (and in particular the implication (iii)
\Longrightarrow
(i)) implies that if
S
is a locally compact semialgebraic set, then one can consider any of the possible definitions of
{\mathcal{S}}^{p}
function on
S
for each
p\geq 1
proposed above and we obtain the same ring
{\mathcal{S}}^{(\infty)}(S)
.
\sqbullet

Theorem
7.10
is still true (as a consequence of a more technical use of
[
BCM
, Cor.1.7]
) when
S
is a general semialgebraic set. As the proof is quite technical, we postpone it until Appendix
A
. Again all possible definitions of
{\mathcal{S}}^{p}
function on a general semialgebraic set
S
proposed above for each
p\geq 1
, provide the same ring
{\mathcal{S}}^{(\infty)}(S)
of smooth semialgebraic functions on
S
.

7.D.2.
Ring of Nash functions

Let
S\subset{\mathbb{R}}^{n}
be a semialgebraic set. By
[
FG
, §(4.10)]
there exist an open semialgebraic
U\subset{\mathbb{R}}^{n}
and a Nash set
X\subset{\mathbb{R}}^{n}
such that if
V\subset U
is an open semialgebraic neighborhood of
S
and
Y\subset V
is the smallest Nash subset of
V
that contains
S
, there exists an open semialgebraic neighborhood
W\subset V
of
S
such that
Y\cap W=X\cap W
.

As
{\mathcal{N}}(X\cap V)={\mathcal{N}}(V)/{\mathcal{I}}^{\bullet}(X\cap V)
for each open semialgebraic neighborhood
V
of
S
, we deduce (using again
[
FGR
, Thm.1.3]
) that
{\mathcal{N}}(S)=\displaystyle\lim_{\longrightarrow}{\mathcal{N}}(X\cap V)
where
V\subset{\mathbb{R}}^{n}
covers the open semialgebraic neighborhoods of
S
. By Remark
2.12
(i) we have
{\mathcal{I}}^{\bullet}(X\cap V)={\mathcal{I}}^{\bullet}(X){\mathcal{N}}(V)
, so
{\mathcal{N}}(X\cap V)={\mathcal{N}}(V)/{\mathcal{I}}^{\bullet}(X){\mathcal{N}}(V)
for each open semialgebraic neighborhood
V
of
S
and
{\mathcal{N}}(S)=\displaystyle\lim_{\longrightarrow}{\mathcal{N}}(V)/{\mathcal{I}}^{\bullet}(X){\mathcal{N}}(V)
where
V\subset{\mathbb{R}}^{n}
covers the open semialgebraic neighborhoods of
S
. Thus,

{\mathcal{N}}(S)=H^{0}(S,({\mathcal{N}}_{{\mathbb{R}}^{n}}/{\mathcal{I}}_{X}^{\bullet})|_{S}).

(7.6)

7.D.3.
Smooth semialgebraic functions versus Nash functions

Let
U
be the open semialgebraic set and
X\subset U
the Nash set described at the beginning of §
7.D.2
. We have
{\mathcal{I}}_{X,x}^{\bullet}\subset{\mathcal{J}}_{S,x}^{\bullet}
for each
x\in S
. Define

A(S):=\{x\in S:\ {\mathcal{I}}_{X,x}^{\bullet}\neq{\mathcal{J}}_{S,x}^{\bullet}\}.

(7.7)

If
A(S)=\varnothing
, then
{\mathcal{I}}_{X,x}^{\bullet}={\mathcal{J}}_{S,x}^{\bullet}
for each
x\in S
and

{\mathcal{S}}^{(\infty)}(S)={\mathcal{C}}^{{\mathcal{N}}}(S)=H^{0}(S,{\mathcal{N}}_{{\mathbb{R}}^{n}}/{\mathcal{J}}_{S}^{\bullet})=H^{0}(S,({\mathcal{N}}_{{\mathbb{R}}^{n}}/{\mathcal{I}}_{X}^{\bullet})|_{S})={\mathcal{N}}(S).

An enlightening example of the previous situation is the semialgebraic set
S:=\{x^{2}-zy^{2}=0,z\geq 0\}
(Whitney’s umbrella with the handle erased), which has
A(S)=\varnothing
. It is natural to wonder what happens if
A(S)\neq\varnothing
. The following result solves this situation.

Theorem 7.12
.

Let
S\subset{\mathbb{R}}^{n}
be a semialgebraic set. Then
{\mathcal{N}}(S)={\mathcal{C}}^{{\mathcal{N}}}(S)
if and only if

A(S)=\varnothing
.

Proof.

It only remains to prove:
If
A(S)\neq\varnothing
, then
{\mathcal{N}}(S)\subsetneq{\mathcal{C}}^{{\mathcal{N}}}(S)
. We distinguish two cases:

Case 1.
We prove first:
if
{\mathcal{J}}_{X,y}^{\bullet}\neq{\mathcal{J}}_{S,y}^{\bullet}
for some
y\in S
, then
{\mathcal{N}}(S)\subsetneq{\mathcal{C}}^{{\mathcal{N}}}(S)
.

Pick a point
y:=(y_{1},\ldots,y_{n})\in S
such that
{\mathcal{J}}_{X,y}^{\bullet}\neq{\mathcal{J}}_{S,y}^{\bullet}
. Then
Y_{y}=\mathcal{Z}({\mathcal{J}}_{S,y}^{\bullet})\subsetneq\mathcal{Z}({\mathcal{J}}_{X,y}^{\bullet})=X_{y}
. Let
U^{y}\subset{\mathbb{R}}^{n}
be an open semialgebraic neighborhood of
y
and let
Y
be a Nash subset of
U^{y}
that is a representative of
Y_{y}
. Let
h\in{\mathcal{O}}(U^{y})
be a Nash equation of
Y
in
U^{y}
. By the Nash curve selection lemma
[
BCR
, Prop.8.1.13]
there exists a Nash arc
\alpha:(-1,1)\to X
such that
\alpha(0)=y
and
\alpha((-1,1)\setminus\{0\})\subset(X\setminus Y)\cap U^{y}
. Let
k:=\omega(h\circ\alpha)
be the order of
h\circ\alpha
at
0
and define
P_{k}:=({\tt x}_{1}-y_{1})^{2k}+\cdots+({\tt x}_{n}-y_{n})^{2k}
. Observe that the order
\omega(P_{k}\circ\alpha)
of
P_{k}\circ\alpha
at
0
is greater than or equal to
2k
. Observe that
P_{k}
has no multiple factors, because the complex analytic set
\mathcal{Z}(\frac{\partial P_{k}}{\partial{\tt x}_{1}},\cdots,\frac{\partial P_{k}}{\partial{\tt x}_{n}})=\{(0,\ldots,0)\}
. Consider the finite sheaf of
{\mathcal{N}}_{{\mathbb{R}}^{n}}
-ideals
{\mathcal{F}}:=P_{k}{\mathcal{N}}_{{\mathbb{R}}^{n}}
on
{\mathbb{R}}^{n}
. As the support of
{\mathcal{F}}
is
\{y\}
, the Nash function
h
defines an element of
H^{0}({\mathbb{R}}^{n},{\mathcal{N}}_{{\mathbb{R}}^{n}}/{\mathcal{F}})
. By Theorem
2.7
(B) there exists a Nash function
h_{k}\in{\mathcal{N}}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})
that represents
h
as an element of
H^{0}({\mathbb{R}}^{n},{\mathcal{N}}_{{\mathbb{R}}^{n}}/{\mathcal{F}})
. This means that there exists a Nash function
a
on
U^{y}
such that
h_{k}-h=aP_{k}
on
U^{y}
. Consider the meromorphic Nash function
\xi_{k}:=\frac{h_{k}}{P_{k}}
.

Observe that
\xi_{k}
is Nash on
{\mathbb{R}}^{n}\setminus\{y\}
. As
h
is an Nash equation of
Y
on
U^{y}
and
h_{k}-h=aP_{k}
on
U^{y}
, we deduce
\xi_{k}=a
on
Y
, so
\xi_{k}
is Nash on
Y
. This means that
\xi_{k}
is local Nash on
S
, that is, an element of
{\mathcal{C}}^{{\mathcal{N}}}(S)
. However,
\xi_{k}
is not an element of
{\mathcal{N}}(S)=H^{0}(S,{\mathcal{N}}_{{\mathbb{R}}^{n}}/{\mathcal{I}}_{X}^{\bullet})
.

The restriction
\xi_{k}|_{X}
is not a Nash function at
y
, because
\omega(P_{k}\circ\alpha)\geq 2k
, whereas
\omega(h_{k}\circ\alpha)=\omega(h\circ\alpha-(a\circ\alpha)(P_{k}\circ\alpha))=\omega(h\circ\alpha)=k
.

Case 2.
We assume in the following
{\mathcal{J}}_{S}^{\bullet}={\mathcal{J}}_{X}^{\bullet}|_{S}
. Let
y\in S
be such that
({\mathcal{J}}_{X}^{\bullet}|_{S})_{y}={\mathcal{J}}_{S,y}^{\bullet}\neq{\mathcal{I}}^{\bullet}_{X,y}
and pick
h_{y}\in{\mathcal{J}}_{X,y}^{\bullet}\setminus{\mathcal{I}}^{\bullet}_{X,y}
. Let
U
be an open semialgebraic neighborhood
U
of
S
in
{\mathbb{R}}^{n}
such that
X\cap U
is the Nash closure of
S
in
U
. By Theorem
6.7
taking
Y=\{y\}
there exist an open semialgebraic neighborhood
U
of
S
in
{\mathbb{R}}^{n}
an a meromorphic Nash function
\xi:U\dashrightarrow{\mathbb{R}}
on
U
such that
\xi|_{X}
is a local Nash and the obstruction of
\xi
to have a Nash extension to
U
concentrates at
y\in S
, Consequently,
\xi|_{S}\in{\mathcal{C}}^{{\mathcal{N}}}(S)\setminus{\mathcal{N}}(S)
, as required.
∎

Appendix A

Proof of Theorem
7.10
for general semialgebraic sets

We prove next Theorem
7.10
for general semialgebraic sets as a byproduct of the results
[
BCM
]
. We explain in certain points how one should interpret and adapt the results in
[
BCM
]
in order to prove implication (iii)
\Longrightarrow
(i) of Theorem
7.10
. The proofs of the implications (i)
\Longrightarrow
(ii) and (ii)
\Longrightarrow
(iii) of Theorem
7.10
proposed above do not use that
S
is locally compact. We denote
{\mathcal{B}}(x,\varepsilon)
the open ball of center
x\in{\mathbb{R}}^{n}
and radius
\varepsilon>0
.

Proof of implication (iii)
\Longrightarrow
(i) of Theorem
7.10
for general semialgebraic sets.

The proof is conducted in several steps:

Step 1.

For each
p\geq 1
there exists a closed semialgebraic set
C_{p}\subset\operatorname{Cl}(S)\setminus S
such that
f
admits a
{\mathcal{C}}^{p}
differentiable extension
H_{p}
to
{\mathbb{R}}^{n}\setminus C_{p}
, the restriction
H_{p}|_{\operatorname{Cl}(S)\setminus C_{p}}
is semialgebraic and
C_{p}\subset C_{p+1}
.

As
f
is local Nash at each point
x\in S
, we deduce that
f
is locally Lipschitz at each point
x\in S
. Let
S_{0}\subset\operatorname{Cl}(S)
be the set of points
x\in\operatorname{Cl}(S)
such that
f
is locally Lipschitz in the intersection with
S
of a neighborhood of
x
, that is, there exist
\varepsilon>0
and
K_{x}>0
(depending on
x
) satisfying

|f(y_{1})-f(y_{2})|<K_{x}\|y_{1}-y_{2}\|

for each
y_{1},y_{2}\in M\cap{\mathcal{B}}(x,\varepsilon)
. Thus,
S_{0}
is an open semialgebraic subset of
\operatorname{Cl}(S)
that contains
S
. For each point
x\in S_{0}
there exists an open semialgebraic neighborhood
V^{x}\subset S_{0}\subset\operatorname{Cl}(S)
of
x
such that
f|_{V^{x}\cap S}
is Lipschitz, so in particular
f|_{V^{x}\cap S}
is uniformly continuous. This means that
f|_{V^{x}\cap S}
admits a unique continuous extension to
\operatorname{Cl}(V^{x}\cap S)
for each
x\in S_{0}
. Consequently, there exists a (unique) continuous extension
F
of
f
to
S_{0}
. The function
F
is semialgebraic, because its graph
\Gamma(F)=\operatorname{Cl}(\Gamma(f))\cap(S_{0}\times{\mathbb{R}})
, which is a semialgebraic set.

As
S_{0}
is locally compact, we may assume from the beginning that
S
is closed in
{\mathbb{R}}^{n}
(using Mostowski’s Lemma
[
Mo
, Lem.6]
as we have done before) and
f
extends continuously to
\operatorname{Cl}(S)
(as a semialgebraic function). We denote
F:\operatorname{Cl}(S)\to{\mathbb{R}}
such extension. Fix a point
x\in S
. As
f
is local Nash at
x
, there exists an open semialgebraic neighborhood
W^{x}\subset{\mathbb{R}}^{n}
of
x
and a Nash function
F_{x}
on
W^{x}
such that
F_{x}|_{S\cap W^{x}}=f|_{S\cap W^{x}}
. As
f
extend to
\operatorname{Cl}(M)
, we deduce
F_{x}|_{\operatorname{Cl}(S)\cap W^{x}}=F|_{\operatorname{Cl}(S)\cap W^{x}}
.

For each
p\geq 1
define
S_{p}\subset\operatorname{Cl}(S)
as the set of points
x\in\operatorname{Cl}(S)
such that there exist
\varepsilon>0
and
K_{x}>0
depending on
x
and (finitely many) continuous semialgebraic functions
F_{\alpha}:\operatorname{Cl}(S)\cap{\mathcal{B}}(x,\varepsilon)\to{\mathbb{R}}
for each
\alpha\in{\mathbb{N}}^{n}
with
|\alpha|\leq p
such that

\Big|F(y_{1})-\sum_{|\alpha|\leq p}\frac{F_{\alpha}(y_{2})}{\alpha!}(y_{1}-y_{2})^{\alpha}\Big|<K_{x}\|y_{1}-y_{2}\|^{p+1}

for each
y_{1},y_{2}\in\operatorname{Cl}(S)\cap{\mathcal{B}}(x,\varepsilon)
. As
f
is local Nash at each point of
S
, we deduce that
S\subset S_{p}
. We claim:
S_{p}
is an open semialgebraic neighborhood of
S
in
\operatorname{Cl}(S)
.

To prove the semialgebraicity of
S_{p}
we only have to show that the existence of a continuous semialgebraic function
F_{\alpha}
on
\operatorname{Cl}(S)\cap{\mathcal{B}}(x,\varepsilon)
is a semialgebraic condition:

•

Semialgebraicity.
There exists polynomials
P_{i},Q_{ij}\in{\mathbb{R}}[{\tt x}]
such that for each
y\in\operatorname{Cl}(S)\cap{\mathcal{B}}(x,\varepsilon)
there exists
z\in{\mathbb{R}}
satisfying
(y,z)\in\bigcup_{i=1}^{\ell}\{P_{i}=0,Q_{i1}>0,\ldots,Q_{iq}>0\}
and if
z^{\prime}\in{\mathbb{R}}
satisfies
(y,z^{\prime})\in\bigcup_{i=1}^{\ell}\{P_{i}=0,Q_{i1}>0,\ldots,Q_{iq}>0\}
, then
z=z^{\prime}
. We denote
F_{\alpha}(y):=z
.

•

Continuity.
For each
y\in\operatorname{Cl}(S)\cap{\mathcal{B}}(x,\varepsilon)
and each
\lambda>0
there exists
\delta>0
such that
y^{\prime}\in\operatorname{Cl}(S)\cap{\mathcal{B}}(x,\varepsilon)
and
\|y^{\prime}-y\|<\delta
implies
|F_{\alpha}(y)-F_{\alpha}(y^{\prime})|<\lambda
.

By
[
KP2
,
Th
]
the function
F|_{\operatorname{Cl}(M)\cap{\mathcal{B}}(x,\varepsilon)}
extends to
{\mathcal{B}}(x,\varepsilon)
as a
{\mathcal{C}}^{p}
semialgebraic function for each
x\in S_{p}
. Consequently, using a
{\mathcal{C}}^{\infty}
partition of unity, we deduce that
f
extends as a
{\mathcal{C}}^{p}
differentiable function
h_{p}
to an open neighborhood
V_{p}\subset{\mathbb{R}}^{n}\setminus C_{p}
of
S_{p}
. Denote
C_{p}:=\operatorname{Cl}(S_{p})\setminus S_{p}=\operatorname{Cl}(S)\setminus S_{p}
, which is a closed semialgebraic subset of
{\mathbb{R}}^{n}
, because
S_{p}
is locally compact. Let
\sigma_{p}:{\mathbb{R}}^{n}\setminus C_{p}\to[0,1]
be a
{\mathcal{C}}^{\infty}
bump function such that
\sigma_{p}|_{S_{p}}=1
and
\sigma_{p}|_{({\mathbb{R}}^{n}\setminus C_{p})\setminus V_{p}}=0
. Define
H_{p}:=\sigma_{p}h_{p}
and extend it by zero to
({\mathbb{R}}^{n}\setminus C_{p})\setminus V_{p}
. Then
H_{p}
is a
{\mathcal{C}}^{p}
differentiable extension of
f
to
{\mathbb{R}}^{n}\setminus C_{p}
and the restriction
H_{p}|_{\operatorname{Cl}(S)\setminus C_{p}}=H_{p}|_{S^{p}}=F|_{S_{p}}
is a semialgebraic function.

By definition
S_{p+1}\subset S_{p}
, so
C_{p}=\operatorname{Cl}(S)\setminus S_{p}\subset\operatorname{Cl}(S)\setminus S_{p+1}=C_{p+1}
for each
p\geq 1
.

Step 2.

Construction of the function
t
that control the loss of differentiability.
By
[
BM2
, Thm.5.1 & Rem.5.2]
(adapted to the semialgebraic case) there exists a Nash manifold
N
of the same dimension as
\operatorname{Cl}(S)
and a proper Nash map
\psi:N\to\operatorname{Cl}(S)
such that
\psi(N)=\operatorname{Cl}(S)
. By
[
BCM
, Cor.1.7]
and its proof applied to the semialgebraic function
F:\operatorname{Cl}(S)\to{\mathbb{R}}
(and
\psi:N\to\operatorname{Cl}(S)
) there exists a function
t:{\mathbb{N}}\to{\mathbb{N}}
such that if
H:{\mathbb{R}}^{n}\to{\mathbb{R}}
is a
{\mathcal{C}}^{t(p)}
differentiable extension of
F
to
{\mathbb{R}}^{n}
, there exists a
{\mathcal{S}}^{p}
function
G:{\mathbb{R}}^{n}\to{\mathbb{R}}
such that
G|_{\operatorname{Cl}(S)}=F
. The function
t
depends by
[
BCM
, Rem.5.5 & §5, p.19]
on:

(1)

The Chevalley function
R:\operatorname{Cl}(S)\times{\mathbb{N}}\to{\mathbb{N}}
that itself depends on
\operatorname{Cl}(S)
, the Nash function
\varphi
and
F:\operatorname{Cl}(S)\to{\mathbb{R}}
(see
[
BCM
, Rem.5.5]
). For each
p\geq 1
, the function
C(\cdot,p)
is uniformly bounded on
N
for each
p\geq 1
, because we are in the semialgebraic setting
[
BCM
, §6, p.22]
. Define
r(p):=\max\{R(b,p):\ b\in\operatorname{Cl}(M)\}
for each
p\geq 1
.

(2)

The exponent
\rho
of regularity of
\operatorname{Cl}(S)
, which exists by
[
BCM
, Lem.3.5]
.

(3)

The maximum
\sigma
of the Łojasiewicz exponents of formulas that involves certain map
M^{s}_{\varphi}
, which depends on
\varphi
and
R
, and the distances to the boundaries of certain strata
\Gamma
of a suitable (finite) stratification of
N
. The quoted formulas are of the type
|\det(M^{s}_{\varphi})(\cdot)|\geq C_{\Gamma}{\rm dist}(\cdot,\partial\Gamma)^{\sigma}
for some constants
C_{\Gamma}>0
(see
[
BCM
, Eq.(10), p.18]
).

A possible choice for
t
is
t(p)=c(\rho p)+\sigma
, see
[
BCM
, p.19]
.

Step 3.

Restriction of
t
to open semialgebraic neighborhoods of
S
in
\operatorname{Cl}(S)
.

If
S^{\prime}\subset\operatorname{Cl}(S)
is an open semialgebraic neighborhood of
S
, it holds that the corresponding function
t^{\prime}\leq t
, because:

•

The Chevalley function
R_{S^{\prime}}
of
S^{\prime}\times{\mathbb{N}}
is the restriction of
R
to
S^{\prime}\times{\mathbb{N}}
.

•

The exponent
\rho_{S^{\prime}}
of regularity of
S^{\prime}
is smaller than or equal to
\rho
.

•

The Łojasiewicz exponents in this case are
\leq\sigma
, because they appear in formulas that involve
M^{s}_{\varphi}|_{\varphi^{-1}(S^{\prime})}
, which depends on
\varphi|_{\varphi^{-1}(S^{\prime})}:\varphi^{-1}(S^{\prime})\to S^{\prime}
and
R_{S^{\prime}}=R|_{S^{\prime}\times{\mathbb{N}}}
, and the distances to the boundaries of certain strata
\Gamma^{\prime}
of a suitable stratification of
\varphi^{-1}(S^{\prime})
. Each stratum
\Gamma^{\prime}
can be chosen contained in some stratum
\Gamma
of the stratification of
N
chosen in (3) above. The quoted formulas are of the type
|\det(M^{s}_{\varphi|_{\varphi^{-1}(S^{\prime})}})(\cdot)|\geq C_{\Gamma^{\prime}}{\rm dist}(\cdot,\partial\Gamma^{\prime})^{\sigma}
for some constants
C_{\Gamma^{\prime}}>0
(see
[
BCM
, Eq.(10), p.18]
).

Consequently, we can take
t^{\prime}
equal to
t
in this case.

Step 4.

Smoothness of
f
.

Let
C^{\prime}:=\operatorname{Cl}(S)\setminus S^{\prime}
and let
h^{\prime}:{\mathbb{R}}^{n}\to{\mathbb{R}}
be a continuous semialgebraic function such that
\mathcal{Z}(h^{\prime})=C^{\prime}
and the restriction
h^{\prime}|_{{\mathbb{R}}^{n}\setminus C^{\prime}}
is a Nash function (Mostowski’s Lemma
[
Mo
, Lem.6]
). Consider the Nash diffeomorphism

\varphi^{\prime}:{\mathbb{R}}^{n}\setminus C^{\prime}\to M^{\prime}:=\{(x,t)\in{\mathbb{R}}^{n+1}:\ h^{\prime}(x)t-1=0\}\subset{\mathbb{R}}^{n+1}.

By
[
BCR
, Cor.8.9.5]
there exist an open semialgebraic neighborhood
U^{\prime}
of
M^{\prime}
in
{\mathbb{R}}^{n+1}
and a Nash retraction
\rho^{\prime}:U^{\prime}\to M^{\prime}
. Let
\sigma^{\prime}:{\mathbb{R}}^{n+1}\to[0,1]
be a
{\mathcal{C}}^{\infty}
bump function such that
\sigma^{\prime}|_{M^{\prime}}=1
and
\sigma^{\prime}|_{{\mathbb{R}}^{n+1}\setminus U^{\prime}}=0
. Suppose that
g|_{S^{\prime}}
extends to a
{\mathcal{C}}^{t(p)}
differentiable function
H^{\prime}
on
{\mathbb{R}}^{n}\setminus C^{\prime}
. Thus,
G^{\prime}:=\sigma^{\prime}\cdot(H^{\prime}\circ\varphi^{\prime-1}\circ\rho^{\prime}):{\mathbb{R}}^{n+1}\to{\mathbb{R}}
is a
{\mathcal{C}}^{t(p)}
differentiable function on
{\mathbb{R}}^{n+1}
, after extending it by
0
outside
U^{\prime}
. If
T^{\prime}:=\varphi(S^{\prime})
, we have
G^{\prime}|_{T^{\prime}}=g\circ\varphi^{\prime-1}|_{T^{\prime}}
. By
[
BCM
, Cor.1.7]
and the discussing above there exists an
{\mathcal{S}}^{p}
function
G_{p}:{\mathbb{R}}^{n+1}\to{\mathbb{R}}
such that
G_{p}|_{T^{\prime}}=f\circ\varphi^{\prime-1}|_{T^{\prime}}
. Define
F_{p}:=G_{p}\circ\varphi^{\prime}:{\mathbb{R}}^{n}\setminus C^{\prime}\to{\mathbb{R}}
, which is a
{\mathcal{S}}^{p}
function such that
F_{p}|_{S}=f
.

If we apply recursively the previous strategy to
S^{\prime}:=S_{t(p)}=\operatorname{Cl}(S)\setminus C_{p}
and
H^{\prime}:=H_{t(p)}
for each
p\geq 1
, we conclude
f\in\bigcap_{p\geq 0}{\mathcal{S}}^{p}(S)={\mathcal{S}}^{(\infty)}(S)
, as required.
∎
